# The backlog

Everything the swarm of 2026-10-14 found and still owes, in one place: about eighty reports and hand-overs, the bodies of pull requests up to #82 and their comments, read in full and de-duplicated, plus what owners have told triage since. Kept by triage (B8).
**State: 18:23 UTC, trunk at `31347b039`.** Generated from triage's data, so send corrections to triage rather than editing a line here: it would be overwritten.

**Severity**, by the owner's priorities: **1** a window lost, scrambled or stuck · **2** a crash or a hang · **3** input stolen or delayed · **4** something jumps or appears during a call or a share · **5** displays · **6** the rest.
**Status**: *open* (nobody has it) · *in #n* (fixed by a pull request that is not on the trunk yet) · *partly* · *on the trunk* (section 4, with whether triage has seen it hold).
**Size**: S up to an hour · M hours · L a feature. **Owner** is a role from the swarm's directory. "Free, give me work" goes to triage.

Both sightings came after clients had given up at 300 s, which e7516784 (1800 s) now keeps from happening<br>*Triage:* B5 (queued 15:46:06): ITS CRASH IS NOT THE SAME STACK: "Swift/ContiguousArrayBuffer.swift:695: Fatal error: Index out of range": ControlSocketTests.watchPrintsEventsAsTheyHappenAndExitsWithZeroWhenArborQuits: after its expectations at 744, 745, 748 had failed the next line is `guard case .view(let view) = events[6]` on an array with fewer than seven events: THE TEST ITSELF indexes past the end, and that trap kills the whole runner and hides every other result. So R5's stack has ONE sighting \| B1 (queued 15:55:16), A SECOND READING OF IPC/ControlServer.swift, all 375 lines, on c1adfe81: RIGHT: every suspend has its resume (the listener's rest when no descriptor is left; the write source born suspended, resumed before it is cancelled); a descriptor is closed only when both its sources have let go. B5 (queued 15:45:21): "one of them crashing the runner"; alone 39 of 39, four times. READ BY TRIAGE at 15:47 on c0717e6e: THE PRODUCT CODE OBEYS THE RULE (close a descriptor only in its source's cancel handler). SERVER (ControlServer.swift): the listener is released in its cancel handler (:83); a connection has a read and a write source on ONE descriptor (:208-209); both cancel handlers call sourceLetGo(), which releases only when BOTH have let go; hangUp() only shuts the socket down and resumes the write source before cancelling it. CLIENT (ArborIPC/ControlClient.swift, send): one `defer { close(fd) }`, no second close on the timeout path, no DispatchSource. First filed at severity 2 as a question; 4 after the reading: a crash of the test runner, not shown in the app; on a Mac the suite has passed at every Mac build today |

(So triage's guess of 15:48, "another road than Events.swift:42", was wrong: it IS that road, and the note is written again after it.) Sent to R5 with a one-line cure. It reddens strict gates of any train whose new settings or commands shift the chaos walks (seed 32 on train/T5-1). B16 saw the same (grab + close) \| B17 (queued 16:21:40): 162 of 9,000 seeds on c1adfe81; nearly all shrink to the same four lines (grab, close). By reading: windowDestroyed clears chosenOnShape[id] (Controller+Events.swift:42) BEFORE the window leaves its workspace; the drag that ends with the window then calls noteWhereItsUserLeftIt (Controller+Layout.swift:550), which still finds it floating and writes the note again. WHY IT MATTERS: it ends 1 walk in 55 at its first step of that kind, so EVERY HUNT ON THE CANDIDATE SEES THAT MUCH LESS of what lies behind it; `make check`'s forty seeds happen to miss it. Cure: clear it after the drag has been let go, or let the note ask `windows[window] != nil` \| H7 (queued 16:44:10): WHY IT MATTERS IN THE PUBLIC REPOSITORY: any change of behaviour shifts the committed seeds; on one train seed 32 meets it; ChaosTests records "an unknown chaos script" as a known issue, the tests PASS, and `make parity` then says "BROKEN A80: test randomActionsKeepEveryInvariant not found" and exits 2: whoever gates a patch and sees that: it is this, not the patch | open | R5 | S | C3 (queued 15:46:32). ## 3. At a Mac

Nearly every real-window script written for this release has never run on a Mac. Pull requests were compiled and unit-tested on a Mac, which settles "does it build" and nothing below.
"Safe" is the verdict of the keeper of the Mac shell harness: the table "On a person's own Mac" in `docs/TESTING.md`. A script that is not in the table keeps what its author says.

### 3a. Scripts, in order of value: the first hour on a hosted Mac, or one sitting

On GitHub's Macs: `scripts/mac-shell/run.sh scripts/mac-shell/<script>` from a checkout of the branch. On a person's Mac: `bash scripts/mac-shell/<script>` in a terminal with Accessibility and Screen Recording.

"Rehearsed" is C1's run of the script on Linux against the fake Mac (real controller, bash 3.2), as ✔/✘: a script with ✘ there would waste its first run on a Mac. Every fault is with the script's owner.

## 5. Owed between branches, and proposals

Follow-ups one branch asked of another, and ideas nobody has taken. The first group breaks a build or brings a bug back if forgotten at a merge.
